La Champions League torna protagonista a San Siro, dove mercoledì 25 novembre, alle ore 21, l'Inter di Cristian Chivu ospiterà lo Stoccarda nella quinta giornata della League Phase. I nerazzurri arrivano all'appuntamento dopo il rocambolesco 4-4 sul campo dell'Atalanta, una partita che ha confermato il potenziale offensivo della squadra, ma anche alcune difficoltà nella gestione delle situazioni difensive. In Europa l'Inter cerca invece la terza vittoria consecutiva, dopo i successi contro Shakhtar Donetsk e Feyenoord, per rilanciare le proprie ambizioni di qualificazione diretta agli ottavi. Riflettori puntati soprattutto sulle scelte offensive di Chivu, chiamato a gestire il rientro di Lautaro Martínez e le condizioni di Marcus Thuram, con Francesco Pio Esposito pronto a giocarsi una maglia da titolare. Tre soluzioni differenti per caratteristiche, ma tutte potenzialmente importanti contro una formazione tedesca che tende a giocare con coraggio e a lasciare spazi nelle transizioni. Sul fronte opposto, attenzione soprattutto a Deniz Undav ed Ermedin Demirović, principali riferimenti offensivi della squadra di Sebastian Hoeneß, senza dimenticare la qualità di Bilal El Khannouss sulla trequarti. Anche gli inserimenti dei centrocampisti e le situazioni da palla inattiva potrebbero offrire occasioni ai protagonisti meno attesi.

Se sei un nuovo utente per confrontare in maniera più completa le quote può essere utile analizzare anche l'eventuale bonus applicato dagli stessi operatori sui nuovi iscritti.

Tra i possibili protagonisti della notte europea di San Siro c'è Francesco Pio Esposito, pronto a ritagliarsi un ruolo importante nell'attacco dell'Inter di Cristian Chivu. Il giovane centravanti nerazzurro rappresenta una soluzione particolarmente interessante per le sue caratteristiche fisiche, la capacità di proteggere il pallone e la pericolosità all'interno dell'area di rigore. Contro uno Stoccarda che predilige un calcio propositivo e tende a lasciare spazi agli avversari, Esposito potrebbe trovare occasioni per sfruttare i rifornimenti provenienti dalle corsie esterne. Il gol di Pio Esposito viene proposto a quota 1.70 da Planetwin365, 1.82 da StarVegas, 1.85 da Eurobet e 1.94 da StarCasinò. Un confronto che evidenzia valutazioni differenti tra gli operatori, con StarCasinò che presenta la quota più alta tra quelle analizzate.

Se sei un nuovo utente per confrontare in maniera più completa le quote può essere utile analizzare anche l'eventuale bonus applicato dagli stessi operatori sui nuovi iscritti.

Una possibilità meno scontata nel mercato dei marcatori riguarda Federico Dimarco, uno dei giocatori più importanti nello sviluppo offensivo dell'Inter. L'esterno nerazzurro rappresenta una risorsa preziosa per la qualità dei cross, la precisione sui calci piazzati e la capacità di rendersi pericoloso anche con conclusioni dalla distanza. In una partita nella quale lo Stoccarda potrebbe concedere spazi sulle corsie laterali, Dimarco potrebbe avere l'opportunità di accompagnare l'azione e cercare direttamente la porta avversaria. La sua eventuale rete rappresenta un'ipotesi meno frequente rispetto a quella di un attaccante, ma trova comunque spazio nelle valutazioni dei bookmaker. Il gol di Dimarco è quotato 3.85 da Planetwin365, 3.95 da StarVegas, 4.20 da Eurobet e 4.60 da StarCasinò. Anche in questo caso è StarCasinò a proporre la quota più elevata, con una differenza significativa rispetto agli altri operatori.

#2165. Calhanoglu, stop con l’Atalanta: l’Inter prova a recuperarlo per la Lazio

Gli accertamenti a Rozzano hanno rilevato un problema ai flessori della coscia destra; non sono indicati i tempi di recupero.

Hakan Calhanoglu ha riportato un risentimento muscolare ai flessori della coscia destra. È l’esito degli esami svolti in mattinata dal centrocampista dell’Inter dopo lo stop contro l’Atalanta. I nerazzurri proveranno a recuperarlo per la Supercoppa del 22 dicembre contro la Lazio. La notizia è riportata da Gianlucadimarzio.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Il centrocampista si è sottoposto agli accertamenti strumentali presso l’Istituto Clinico Humanitas di Rozzano. Nel comunicato, l’Inter precisa: «Hakan Çalhanoğlu si è sottoposto questa mattina a esami strumentali presso l'Istituto Clinico Humanitas di Rozzano. Gli esami hanno evidenziato un risentimento muscolare ai flessori della coscia destra».

Calhanoglu era uscito nel primo tempo della partita contro l’Atalanta per un problema alla coscia destra. Come riportato da Sky Sport, il 24 novembre non si è allenato con il resto della squadra alla vigilia della gara con lo Stoccarda: stava svolgendo gli esami strumentali.

#2164. Brian Madjo, 17, electrifies Villa despite Super Cup defeat by PSG

Unai Emery’s Aston Villa 2.0 remain under construction but from the wreckage of a difficult summer there has emerged a potential star for the future.

Emery may have now suffered four successive defeats in the Super Cup, yet the big positive from a balmy night in Salzburg was the performance of teenage forward Brian Madjo. Aged 17 years and 212 days, Madjo became the youngest player to start and score in this continental showpiece, just over a week after he was given permission to play for his club.

Signed from Metz for £10m in January, Madjo had been prevented from playing because of Fifa’s rules on the international transfer of minors. Villa’s appeal to the Court of Arbitration for Sport to have him registered was upheld on August 4 and what a prospect he appears.

Born in Enfield but raised in Luxembourg, there has been a huge buzz around him since his arrival in the new year.

Paris St-Germain have produced an abundance of talented teenagers over the years and Madjo did not look out of place here.Désiré Doué’s second-half strike secured PSG the Super Cup – contested by the winners of the Champions League and Europa League – for the second successive year, but Emery will have seen enough to give him renewed confidence.

Desire Doue puts PSG 2-1 ahead despite originally being flagged offside 🫣📺 TNT Sports & HBO Max pic.twitter.com/vnMIZCWJaj

His team played with all the resilience and bravery that has become a trademark, epitomised by the powerful Madjo and another teenager in midfielder George Hemmings.

Emery said: “Brian has scored goals for us in pre-season and has shown his capacity to work in our idea. I am so happy because the young players were involved in our work and not playing last season.

“Of course there is still work to do but they showed they can play with us. They have responded fantastically and will then feel the confidence. With a loss you can never be happy but overall I am proud of all the players and everything we’ve done. Some changes will be needed but we’re confident.”

This was a fine Villa performance to silence the doom merchants who have been fearing a troubled season. After the painful sales of Morgan Rogers, Youri Tielemans and Lucas Digne, Villa are clearly a team in transition. They are operating under restrictions to comply with Uefa’s financial rules and there is still the possibility of another important player leaving before the transfer deadline day.

England centre-back Ezri Konsa remains a priority target for Premier League champions Arsenal, and was absent here. Konsa, Emiliano Martínez and Ollie Watkins were all granted extended leave after their participation in the World Cup, which has done little to ease the fears of supporters over more departures. The trio will return to training this weekend.

While some outsiders are predicting a difficult season for Villa, do not bother telling Emery. He has turned putting noses out of joint into an art form since sweeping into Villa Park and with him around anything feels possible.

The Basque remains optimistic about the future and those hopes will only have been underlined by the performance of Madjo. He bullied PSG centre-back Willian Pacho for much of the evening and even managed to steal the limelight from PSG’s magician Khvicha Kvaratskhelia.

Hemmings, 19, has emerged from the academy and already made a real impression on his manager. Despite the high regard for those youngsters, this always appeared likely to be a tough proposition against a full-strength PSG team. Indeed, before this game in Salzburg only 39 per cent of Europa League winners have beaten their Champions League rivals in this fixture.

The French champions did start slowly, but had been cranking up the pressure before taking a 20th minute lead with a spectacular strike from their Georgian superstar.

Doué forced a save from Marco Bizot and, after Villa failed to clear, the loose ball landed in the path of Kvaratskhelia, who cut inside on the left to his right foot before smashing a shot into the roof of the net.

Khvicha Kvaratskhelia puts PSG 1-0 ahead vs Aston Villa 🔥📺 TNT Sports & HBO Max pic.twitter.com/BIf8njpaSx

It was a strike of sheer power and accuracy, catching Bizot by surprise. PSG briefly appeared in control, but Villa responded superbly. In 17 minutes they created six goal-scoring chances before the outstanding Madjo finally made one of them count.

Before his goal, he had narrowly missed the target twice with a pair of headers from John McGinn’s crosses. Villa also had another opportunity 10 minutes before half-time, when Pacho’s mistake allowed Hemmings to run from the centre circle unopposed. As he entered the penalty area, the PSG defenders had raced back to recover and a promising moment ended with Madjo curling a shot wide.

Brian Madjo with a big chance to put Aston Villa level 😮📺 TNT Sports & HBO Max pic.twitter.com/pqsC5Hor15

PSG goalkeeper Matvey Safonov then produced his first save just before the break, turning away Boubacar Kamara’s close-range header. Villa did carve out another golden chance when Madjo’s strength took hm past Pacho, but he hit the outside of the post. Madjo’s moment did come, though, as he once again bullied Pacho to divert a McGinn cross into the roof of the net.

Brian Madjo at 17-years-old scores in the UEFA Super Cup Final 🤯📺 TNT Sports & HBO Max pic.twitter.com/PccX7FxjAr

Villa were buoyant, but it was PSG who scored the crucial second goal after Doué was awarded the goal following a VAR review. Doué was sent clear down the right by substitute Ousmane Dembélé and confidently beat Bizot, with reviews concluding that he had been played onside by Matty Cash.

The game was refereed by Omar Artan, the Somali referee who was supposed to officiate at the World Cup but was denied entry to the United States.

It became rather fractious as time began to tick away, with McGinn booked after complaining over the placement of a free-kick. Madjo was replaced by Tammy Abraham with 17 minutes left and this will be an evening he will never forget.

PSG (4-3-3): Safonov, Hakimi, Marquinhos, Pacho, Mendes (Hernandez 75), Neves (Ruiz 75), Vitinha, Emery, Akliouche, (Dembele 45), Doue (Mayulu 87), Kvaratskhelia (Buraldo 88).Substitutes: Chevalier (g), Zabarnyi, Digne, Fernandez, Barcola, Ndjantou, Mbaye.Villa (4-2-3-1): Bizot, Cash, Lindelof, Torres (Mings 80), Maatsen, Kamara (Bogarde 72), Gomes (Barkley 79), McGinn 7 (Alysson 73), Buendia, Hemmings, Madjo (Abraham 72).Substitutes: Wright (g), Asemota (g), Barkley, Nedeljkovic, Cisse, Rowe, Burrowes, Lynch.Booked: Gomes, Torres, McGinn.Referee: Omar Artan (Somalia).

PSG are your 2026 UEFA Super Cup Champions 🏆📺 TNT Sports & HBO Max pic.twitter.com/1qCKEQ0IPK

Both Bradley Barcola and Ibrahim Mbaye have been heavily linked with moves to Liverpool this summer and neither player came off the bench for PSG tonight. Does that indicate that potentially those moves could be close, with neither played risked of picking up an injury?

PSG play Lens in the Trophée des Champions on Sunday before they kick off the defence of their Ligue 1 crown at home against Rennes.

New Villa signing Alejandro Garnacho has been ruled out tonight as part of the concussion protocols.

Team news is in 🔒 #PSGAVL pic.twitter.com/yeKkyIKiHQ

What will we learn from the “new Aston Villa” tonight? After the sales of Morgan Rogers, Youri Tielemans and Lucas Digne, this is a Villa team in transition.

Unai Emery virtually admitted last night it could be another slow start to the season, like last year. Villa face a full-strength PSG team hoping to win the Super Cup for the second time in a row.

I don’t think we can take much from this result tonight, whatever it is. Villa cannot be properly judged until the window closes. Don’t bother telling that to Unai Emery, though. He is ready to continue defying the doubters.

The Champions League and Europa League winners fight it out in the Uefa Super Cup in Salzburg as PSG take on Aston Villa. PSG took on English opposition in the Super Cup a year ago, when they played Tottenham. PSG fell 2-0 down in that game in Udine but scored two late goals to take it to penalties, where they won 4-3. These sides met in the Champions League knockout stages two seasons ago; PSG did progress from the quarter-final encounter as they went on to win the competition but not without a scare as Aston Villa did win at Villa Park and PSG only progressed 5-4 on aggregate. The two clubs have been involved in a transfer this week, with left-back Lucas Digne re-joining PSG from Aston Villa.

Aston Villa have played in the Super Cup once before back in 1982 when they took in Barcelona. They lost the first leg 1-0 over at Camp Nou but fought back to win the second leg 3-0 at Villa Park to claim the trophy. Aston Villa were last in action out in the Far East last Friday, falling to a 2-1 defeat to Bayern Munich in Hong Kong. It has been a big summer of change at the club, with key starters like Morgan Rogers and Youri Tielemans departing and the likes of Alejandro Garnacho and Joao Gomes arriving.

“I feel like the club is in a new era, with this possibility to fight for trophies,” Unai Emery told Uefa. “We are trying to be successful through, firstly, [building] a winning team, and then aiming for the positive consequences after it; winning trophies. To play the UEFA Super Cup is another opportunity to be there, the opportunity to win a trophy, to play against Paris Saint-Germain; the [best] team in the world now and another team we can compare [ourselves] with.”

Emery has confirmed that Ezri Konsa, Ollie Watkins and Emiliano Martinez will not be involved as they are still on their breaks following the World Cup.

PSG have now won back-to-back Champions Leagues, beating Arsenal in the final last season. They have only played a couple of friendlies in pre-season, losing 3-0 to Mallorca before drawing 1-1 against Manchester United. Luis Enrique’s side will take on Lens in the Trophée des Champions on Sunday before kicking off the defence of their Ligue 1 title at home to Rennes.

“I focus on renewing that desire to win again,” Enrique told Uefa. “It is difficult to keep winning. Right now, we have a unique challenge; something that is practically unprecedented in the history of European football, which is to draw level with Real Madrid if we can win a third consecutive Champions League. No-one in history has won three in a row, only Real Madrid. We can equal them. But I do not think there is any team in Europe more motivated than us.

“No-one rules out PSG any more. Two years ago, it was impossible to put us among the favourites; not impossible, but we were not one of the favourites. Now, no one would say that Paris Saint-Germain are not the favourites. That is something we have earned, but if we are going to maintain that position, we have to work even harder.”

PSG have added Monaco winger Maghnes Akliouche and Digne to their ranks this summer. They are also pushing to sign Ferran Torres from Barcelona and Mika Godts from Ajax. Bradley Barcola and Ibrahim Mbaye have both been linked with moves to Liverpool but have been included in their squad for tonight’s game, as have Akliouche and Digne.

#2161. Caso arbitri e bussate in sala VAR: archiviata l'inchiesta dal tribunale di Monza

Caso arbitri e bussate in sala VAR: archiviata l'inchiesta dal tribunale di Monza

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

"La giudice per le indagini preliminari del Tribunale di Monza Letizia Anna Brambilla ha archiviato l'inchiesta per concorso in frode sportiva sulle presunte “bussate” nella sala Var di Lissone tra il 2024 e il 2025". La giudice ha accolto in toto le argomentazioni della Procura di Monza, che aveva chiesto di non procedere nei confronti del designatore arbitrale e supervisore Gianluca Rocchi, del supervisore degli ufficiali di gara addetti al Var Andrea Gervasoni e degli arbitri addetti al Var Luigi Nasca e Rodolfo Di Vuolo.

Scegli Fcinter1908.it come tuo sito preferito su Google: clicca qui

Si legge sul sito del quotidiano Il Giorno che per primo aveva parlato anche delle richieste di archiviazioni del Tribunale di Milano. Il procedimento era arrivato a Monza per competenza territoriale e ipotizzava alterazioni durante la partita tra Inter e Hellas Verona del 6 gennaio 2024. Nasca e Di Vuolo erano indagati per la mancata raccomandazione di una on field review nell'azione che precedette la rete del 2-1". Rocchi era indagato per le famose bussate durante Udinese-Parma e Gervasoni per un presunto condizionamento in Salernitana-Modena.

Santini e Gittardi, i due pm che si sono occupati della vicenda hanno evidenzato, da come riporta il quotidiano: "criticità istituzionali e organizzative nel settore arbitrale, segnato da tensioni interne, contrapposizioni sulla governance, perplessità sulla regolamentazione della sala Var e rischi derivanti dalla promiscuità dei rapporti tra organi arbitrali, federazione e società calcistiche. Profili che possono - al limite ed eventualmente - assumere rilievo in altre sedi, disciplinare, regolamentare o politico-sportivo, ma non risultano sufficienti a fondare l'esercizio dell'azione penale per frode sportiva".

#2158. Roma e Como guidano le ammende: sanzionate altre sette società

Fiorentina e Milan pagano 5mila euro ciascuna; provvedimenti anche per Atalanta, Genoa, Monza, Udinese e Bologna.

Il Giudice sportivo ha inflitto 10.000 euro di ammenda alla Roma per oggetti lanciati dai suoi sostenitori durante la gara e 8.000 euro al Como per il ritardo di circa quattro minuti nell’inizio del secondo tempo. Secondo quanto riportato da Giudice sportivo, Sanzionate anche Fiorentina, Milan, Atalanta, Genoa, Monza, Udinese e Bologna.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Alla Roma sono contestati il lancio di tre petardi e di un accendino sul terreno di gioco, oltre a un fumogeno e una bottiglietta di plastica nel recinto di gioco. L’ammenda da 10.000 euro è stata attenuata ai sensi dell’articolo 29, comma 1, lettera b) del CGS.

Per l’Atalanta la sanzione è di 4.000 euro. I suoi sostenitori hanno lanciato un accendino sul terreno di gioco e due bottigliette nel recinto di gioco. Anche in questo caso l’ammenda è stata attenuata in base all’articolo 29, comma 1, lettera b) del CGS.

Il Como è stato multato di 8.000 euro a titolo di responsabilità oggettiva per aver provocato ingiustificatamente un ritardo di circa quattro minuti nell’inizio del secondo tempo. Il provvedimento segnala la recidiva reiterata.

Ammende di 5.000 euro ciascuna per Fiorentina e Milan, sempre a titolo di responsabilità oggettiva. La Fiorentina ha causato ingiustificatamente un ritardo di circa due minuti nell’inizio del secondo tempo; il Milan, nella stessa fase della gara, un ritardo di circa tre minuti. Per entrambe è indicata la recidiva.

Il Genoa deve pagare 3.000 euro per aver provocato ingiustificatamente, a titolo di responsabilità oggettiva, un ritardo di circa due minuti nell’inizio del primo tempo. Stessa cifra per il Monza, al quale è contestato un ritardo ingiustificato di circa due minuti nell’inizio della ripresa, sempre a titolo di responsabilità oggettiva.

#2152. Selfie-seekers bring Man Utd vs Leeds match to premature end

Aren’t friendlies supposed to be non-events? Manchester United against Leeds United in Dublin always had the capacity to be lively, but try explaining the half hour that Bruno Fernandes experienced in one breath. It would be impossible.

There was only one player the vast majority of the crowd wanted to see in the grandiosely named Boyle Sports Cup and, in chronological order from his 60th minute introduction, he received a standing ovation, hit the bar, had a goal disallowed, missed a penalty in a shoot-out and lifted a trophy.

Most remarkably – and, certainly, worryingly – of all, he was central to the game being ended early as five young boys, one after the other, breached security and ran to him in the middle of pitch, all of them successfully posing for photos with the Portuguese.

You could say it was harmless fun but it should not have happened and the flummoxed faces that were pulled on the Manchester United bench, as an army in high-visibility jackets stood and watched it all unfold, suggested they took a dim view.

It was a shame because in that cameo, Fernandes showed he has returned from the World Cup in excellent shape, the tempo of United’s play immediately lifting when he had the ball. It is wrong to make conclusions in the summer, but you are on safe ground to state he will be integral to their ambitions.

Croke Park is a stadium steeped in history and another chapter was penned here, as the capacity crowd made this the highest attended soccer match in Ireland with 82,000 coming through the gates: it is a magnificent venue, one that would be well capable of hosting a Champions League final.

Michael Carrick hopes in years to come that is the stage where his team will be but, right now, it is a case of learning to walk again before they can run and he more than anyone will know how far off that target is, not that it will stop him trying to get there.

The one thing that is obvious about the way United play under him is the first thought from everyone is to go forward quickly. Yes, this was only a friendly, but the ambition to be on the front foot and cause problems was evident from the start.

The team in red had much the better of the opening exchanges, with Diogo Dalot having the first shot that went over but, unlike on July 25, when Mayo beat Kerry here in Gaelic football’s All Ireland final, which went viral, nobody was cheering the sight of a ball clearing the bar.

It was like a home game for United and the majority got what they wanted in the 15th minute when a slick counter-attack ended with Bryan Mbuemo sliding a pass into Joshua Zikzee, who rifled past James Trafford with a first-time finish.

Devastating counterattack! 💥Manchester United break and Joshua Zirkzee is there to finish it off 🤩 pic.twitter.com/y6QHbZMi7n

Zirkzee is unlikely to be part of Carrick’s squad beyond the transfer window, with interest from clubs in Italy, and it would be disingenuous to suggest his career at Old Trafford has been successful, but his attitude was exemplary and he played as if he was desperate to change his manager’s mind.

There may have been an anticipation that United would stride clear, but this game was also about Leeds, whose summer has been encouraging and whose confidence from staying up last season has evidently enabled them to blossom.

You would be way off the mark if you include them in your conversations about which clubs are going to be relegated next May, and the sense they showed to stay with Daniel Farke has paid off. They have bought some good players, not least goalkeeper James Trafford and Harry Wilson.

Top stop from James Trafford! 🚫The Leeds United keeper has been busy in the first half... pic.twitter.com/VKmxNVKBsA

Trafford has done the right thing getting away from Manchester City and you could argue that he is already reaping the benefit of playing for a manager who wants him, as he made a fabulous save in the 23rd minute to thwart Mbuemo. It was all part of performance that led to him being named player of the match.

He was involved in the move that brought the Leeds equaliser, too, as his long clearance caused uncertainty and led to Tyler Fletcher being pickpocketed by Dan James and he nudged a pass into Brenden Aaronson, who kept his cool and danced into the area, ignoring Senne Lammens, to finish.

Leeds hit back with their first shot on target! 💥Brenden Aaronson with a cool finish 🥶 pic.twitter.com/5WsSZY41j6

It was an entertaining affair, mercifully lacking the bile that would usually bubble away when it is staged in England, and the longer the game advanced, the better Leeds looked. They finished the opening period strongly and came back after the restart, with 10 changes, in the same fettle.

Farke, whose team had shown their ability to score heavily when they pummelled Liverpool 4-2 in Chicago 10 days ago, would have taken plenty of encouragement, but the perfectionist in him would have cursed the opportunities that were squandered early in the second period.

The misses proved costly as the game meandered and the shoot-out went United’s way, after Wilson and James Justin failed from 12 yards, with Noussair Mazraoui thumping in the decisive kick. It really is never dull.

Manchester United Lammens (Mee 61); Dalot (Mazraoui 60), Yoro, Heaven (Armer 46), Amass (Shaw 60); Santos (Collyer 46), T Fletcher (Tielemans 61); Diallo (J Fletcher 60), Zirkzee (Cunha 60), Dorgu (Thompson 46); Mbuemo (Fernandes 60).Subs not used Heath, Honor, Lacey, Gabriel.

Leeds United Trafford (Cairns 46); Bijol (Rodon 46), Muharemovic (Cresswell 46), Bornauw; Bogle (Gnonto 46), Longstaff (Stach 46), Tanaka (Ampadu 46), Chadwick (Justin 46); James (Okafor 46), Aaronson (Wilson 46); Nmecha (Calvert-Lewin 46).Subs not used Mahady, Lienou, Mills, Piroe.

Decent enough watch for roughly half the game, settled on a shoot-out in which Alex Cairns got nowhere near any of Manchester United’s penalties. Doubt he will lose much sleep over it. Bruno Fernandes grinned broadly when he missed.

Bottom left. Manchester United win the Boyle Sports Cup.

Smacks it even higher into the top right.

And I think Amapdu’s signal means Leeds will go first.

The ref decides to call time as the stewards cannot catch the juvenile intruders.

Not sure what we can say about stadium safety here but the game has ended prematurely after five kids came on, all of whom got to Bruno Fernandes to take selfies. It was very funny but, equally, plenty on the Manchester United bench were far from happy. Penalties to follow.

Some sealion ball juggling from Mazraoui involving foot, thigh and head, gets him past Justin on the right  but Gnonto heads away his cross.

Two young Man Utd fans run on to the pitch to try to take selfies with Man utd players. They are joined by three more and now another.

Bruno Fernandes lets fly from 20 yards, cushioning a pass from Tielemans before hammering his shot into the crossbar with little backlift.

Gnonto breaks down the right, plays it infield to Wilson who runs at the heart of the Man Utd defence but smacks his shot into a defender.

Cresswell blocks Mazroui’s shot with his right elbow which was pinned to his side and the ball falls to Bruno Fernandes but Cresswell is there again to block the shot, this time with his legs. From the corner, Bruno Fernandes balloons a long shot over the bar.

Enormous frustration for the masses who have wanted a Bruno goal. He’s just “scored” - right on the moment when our friend the stadium announcer bellowed the match was a sell out but never provided the actual figure – but a linesman’s flag has stopped him in his tracks. You fancy he will have one more involvement before the game creeps towards a penalty shootout.

Bruno Fernandes shoots past Cairns with a left-foot shot from 16 yards, smacking it inside the left post but the flag had long been raised.

The ball pinballs out to Justin by the D from the corner and he hammers a shot that Mee saves with great agility by the right post.

Excellent pass from Okafor from the left ought to have put Calvert-Lewin through but Armer does brilliantly to hold him up. Gnonto makes a run down the side but Calvert-Lewin checks back to shoot into Armer and the ball deflects behind for a corner.

There will be a shootout if the match is drawn after 90 minutes. The Boyle Sports Cup cannot be shared, it seems.

Mazraoui drives down the right and feeds it inside to Fletcher whose deflected cross falls kindly for Thompson but the young striker cannot control it and Leeds recover.

Amapdu hurls a long throw into the box on the diagonal from the right. Rodon flicks it on and Calvert-Lewin dives to try to nod it past Mee but he can only stick his effort wide. It was just a bit too far in front of him to reach with control and power.

Clever pass from Gnonto sends Wilson into the box on the underlap to cross low, seeking Calvert-Lewin. Mee is out to stop it reaching him. Oakfor sets off on a  bustling run down the left for 35 yards but then fluffs the cross.

Jack Fletcher is playing on the right with Tielemans and Collyer as the central two. Bruno Fernandes and Matheus Cunha are playing centrally with Thompson on the left. Nothing much happening as Leeds keep their defensive shape while Manchester United probe away.

Yoro and Bornauw are the only two starters left. Stach intercepts Matheus Cunha’s cross in from the right.

Understandably Manchester United look disjointed now. Leeds have had longer for their changes to settle.

Seven Manchester United changes: Mee, Tielmans, Shaw, Bruno Fernandes, Jack Fletcher, Matheus Cunha and Mazraoui incoming for Dalot, Lammens, Amass, Mbeumo, Diallo, Zirkzee and Tyler Fletcher.

Dalot slides in to trip Okafor after the Swiss forward was played through the middle by Calvert-Lewin. Stach takes the free-kick from 22 yards, bends it round the wall but Lammens read it well and steps across to catch by the right post.

Zirkzee’s trickery down the right leaves Justin in his wake but having done the hard bit he fluffs the cross, delivering it too high for a shot and too low for a header. Thompson tries to contort himself to reach it but no one is that flexible.

Couple of threatening crosses from Gnonto from the right just elude Calvert-Lewin and Alfie Cresswell, son of Richard, brother of Charlie, follows up the second to shoot from the edge of the box. Lammens gathers comfortably.

Just the 10 half-time changes for Leeds and the worst thing about that was the fact it gave the stadium announcer, who hadn’t grasped that he didn’t need to shout into a loud speaker system, the opportunity to screech unchecked for close to two minutes, leaving everyone with tinnitus. Give the wrong man a microphone and chaos will ensue. Anyway, we are underway in the second period and the one change the majority of the stadium wants to be see is the introduction of Bruno Fernandes. His presence would help United regain some impetus.

Huge cheer as Bruno Fernandes, Luke Shaw et al start to warm up.

Lammens saves from Harry Wilson’s shot from 10 yards after Rodon’s underlapping ran down the right caught Amass off guard.

Senne Lammens to the rescue! 🧤A great reaction save from the Belgian 👏🇧🇪 pic.twitter.com/GRau9yk02n

Thompson tackles Rodon as he tried to lengthen his stride down the right.

Oddly Bornauw, who is poised to join Hamburg on loan, is the only player kept on from the first half for Leeds.

Gnonto, who is playing at right wing-back, is palyed down the line by Rodon and bends over a low cross looking for Calvert-Lewin’s late run. Lammens dives out to smother it.

Manchester United make three changes – Tynan Thompson, Toby Collyer and Dan Armer replace Santos, Heaven and Dorgu.

Cairns; Rodon, Bornauw, Cresswell; Justin, Ampadu, Stach, Gnonto; Wilson, Okafor; Calvert-Lewin.

Pleasant summer watch between largely second string players. Mbeumo looks a class apart from the other attacking players. On this evidence he might be worth a punt for goals and assists topping 25 this season like they did in 2024-25 for Brentford. Though he may have to take pens off Bruno Fernandes to get there.

The ref blows directly on 45 minutes moments after Nmecha rab out of room to control Tanaka’s cross and booted it out for a Man Utd goal-kick.

Manchester United probe patiently for half a minute around the Leeds area but the pass supposed to play Mbeumo through is picked off by Bijol and he plays it out to James who hares off down the left for 50 yards, cuts in on his right after leaving Yoro in his vapour trail, and whacks a shot over the bar from 22 yards.

Yoro hooks Bogle’s long chip out for a throw-in. This time Leeds take it short and try to engineer a crossing opportunity but go back to Trafford when they can’t find space.

Mbeumo’s on fire tonight as he has been all pre-season, causing Leeds problems whenever he runs at the defence. Now he storms down the centre-right and arcs a shot from 18 yards that beats Trafford’s leap and crashes into the underside of the crossbar. Bornauw hacks it away.

Amass tries to play a one-two with Zirkzee but the crucial middle pass of the three, from Zirkzee, goes astray.

Trafford dives to catch Dorgu’s cross. But he then boots his punt out for a throw-in and Tanaka is penalised for tussling with Dorgu who was thrown the ball and set off on a dribble down the inside-right.

Bikol heads Longstaff’s free-kick from the right over the bar after clever work from Bogle to earn the opportunity. .

Manchester United 1 Leeds United 1 (Aaronson) James robs Tyler Fletcher in the inside-left position and sprints towards the 18-yard line before slipping it inside to Aaronson. The once erratic finisher takes a touch to put Heaven on his heels then slots a right-foot shot crisply into the bottom left corner.

Leeds hit back with their first shot on target! 💥Brenden Aaronson with a cool finish 🥶 pic.twitter.com/5WsSZY41j6

Tanaka has two long throws in from the right. Dorgu heads the first one out for the second which is flicked on by Bornauw but Man Utd clear the second ball.

Tyler Fletcher takes a trick out of his old man’s book by grabbing James round the waist once the speed merchant had knocked the ball past him. Leeds go backwards from the free-kick as Man Utd’s press hounds them.

Trafford confidently catches the cross from the corner then bowls it out 40 yards to set Aaronson off on a counter. His solo raid down the right is stymied by Amass’s diligent defending.

Mbeumo drives down the gap between Muharemovic and Bijol, tries to find Doegu but Bornauw slides in to intercept but only succeeds in playing it back to Mbeumo who lashes a left-foot shot that Trafford tips round the right post at full stretch.

Top stop from James Trafford! 🚫The Leeds United keeper has been busy in the first half... pic.twitter.com/VKmxNVKBsA

There is certainly more urgency about Manchester United’s play. Leeds are living dangerously.

In the couple of minutes after the goal Manchester United almost punish Leeds’ high defensive line again but Muharemovic rides to the rescue. Bijol is a very good box defender but he lacks pace.

United’s start has been bright and confident and the system they playing is flipping between 4-4-2 and 4-2-3-1 and the first thought is always to go forward. The goal they have just scored is indicative of how they have started and it’s a positive for Joshua Zirkzee, who has spent much of his time at Old Trafford under a cloud, to have finished it with aplomb. Whether it will be enough to keep him at Old Trafford for the long run remains to be seen, particularly as interest from Italy persists, but what can’t be doubted is his attitude. He looks sharp.

Manchester United 1 Leeds United 0 (Zirkzee) Leeds, on the attack, lose possession down the middle and Dorgu works it out to Mbeumo who beats Bijol 20 yards inside the Man Utd half. He pokes it through the Leeds captain;s legs and hares off down the wing until he gets to the 18 yard line and from there he bends the perfect pass to Zirkzee who sweeps home a shot into the bottom left with his left foot.

Devastating counterattack! 💥Manchester United break and Joshua Zirkzee is there to finish it off 🤩 pic.twitter.com/y6QHbZMi7n

Manchester United build slowly down the right and then Dalot injects some urgency by whipping in a low cross. Zirkzee meets it not so much with a shot but with the timing of his arrival on the ball, deflecting it towards the right of goal powerfully, Trafford gets down well to save.

Leeds cross the free-kick into the box and James’ volley strikes a defender in the midriff and then hits the deck when going for the rebound, bundled over by Yoro. The ref waves play on and Manchester Unted counter with Amad Diallo dribbling down the right until tackled by Chadwick at the price of a corner.

Dorgu is penalised for a scissors-tackle on Bornauw. He did get the ball but only after he had caught he blond defender with his other leg. Carrick leaps up to protest, as does Farke but he is saying that Dorgu has twice slid in wildly.

Leeds defend the corner fairly solidly but Manchester United recover possession 30 yards out and set up Dalot to have a crack with a dipping, swerving effort that Trafford pats down in front of his face and catches after it bounces.

Amad Diallo finds Mbeumo down the right and he tries to head the ball across the box but Muharemovic is well-placed to head it back out but an attempt to nod the ball back to Trafford goes astray and the £45m man can’t stop it going out for a corner.

Leeds free-kick for either a foul by Amass on Aaronson or Dorgu on Bogle. Hard to tell, one was a split-second after the other.

Amass pulls back Bogle after being tackled by the Leeds wing-back and Leeds build up the right from the free-kick, earning themselves a throw-in parallel with the penalty spot. In Ampadu’s absence Tanaka is the trebuchet, hurling in the long throw that Bornauw nods on but Manchester united clear the second ball.

Leeds work the ball back to Taafford from the kick off and he chips it long up the right. Dorgu heads it out for a throw-in. Leeds go backwards again and pass the ball around confidently.

Jaka Bijol captains Leeds, Diogo Dalot leads out Manchester United.

They would be as well delaying the start of the game here the concourses outside this magnificent and historic stadium are packed with crowds taking in the solar eclipse. What a genuinely extraordinary moment! There will be plenty of time later this evening to talk about the football, for now attentions are firmly elsewhere!

🤩 Ready for a first #LUFC start! pic.twitter.com/KU5ica0HcG

And for Senne Lammens, a new shirt number, from 31 to Numero Uno:

Lammens is not the only one. Marcus Rashford wore 39 in his debut season with Man Utd, moved to 19 for the next three then changed to 10 when Zlatan Ibrahimovic left the club. The 10 shirt has been given to Matheus Cunha so now he will wear 14, the number he had at Barcelona in his Liga title-winning season.

Good evening and welcome to live coverage of the pre-season friendly between Manchester United and Leeds United at a sold-out Croke Park in Dublin. Last year Leeds played Milan in the Irish capital and drew a crowd of 52,000 but at the GAA citadel, as I think I’m contractually obliged to call it, there will be 83,000 present, a testament to the Premier League’s reach and these two clubs’ popularity in Ireland founded on the exploits of Billy Whelan, Noel Cantwell, Johnny Giles, Roy Keane, Gary Kelly et al. It’s the first club match ever held at the stadium.

It’s a fixture that always stirs the blood and whenever it comes around you can guarantee that there will be reams of stuff about the War of the Roses, clash in styles, Busby and Revie, Bobby and Jack, large swathes of which this author has been guilty of producing. I’ll spare you all that today because while it’s relevant to fans of both clubs, they know it backwards. And it’s a match that has come around a wee bit too frequently in pre-season recently, first in Perth, Western Australia, in 2019 when Ole Gunnar Solskjaer’s side beat Marcelo Bielsa’s 4-0, then in 2023 in Oslo when Erik ten Haag’s second or third string defeated Daniel Farke’s threadbare, just relegated squad 2-0 and last year in Stockholm, which ended in a 0-0 draw between two scratch sides.

This one comes round later in the pre-season schedule than those three so we can expect to see stronger XIs, at least for part of the game. Leeds’ pre-season has gone well: after a 3-2 defeat by Wrexham in Tampa, they have beaten Sunderland in New Jersey, Liverpool in Chicago and RB Leipzig at Elland Road. Manchester United also lost to Wrexham (in Helsinki) then beat Rosenborg in Trondheim, Atlético in Stockholm and drew one apiece with the European champions, PSG, in Gothenburg last time out.

While Leeds will have new signing James Trafford available and Noah Okafor, who scored twice in Leeds’ first league victory at Old Trafford for 45 years last April, in the squad for the first time after the World Cup, Manchester United have Kobbie Mainoo and Marcus Rashford back in training since the start of the week but it seems too soon for them to start but Youri Tielemans is pushing to make his first start.

#2145. Stoccarda, Hoeness: "Visto Atalanta-Inter live, insoliti i gol subiti. Vedere San Siro..."

Stoccarda, Hoeness: "Visto Atalanta-Inter live, insoliti i gol subiti. Vedere San Siro..."

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

Sebastian Hoeness, allenatore dello Stoccarda, ha parlato in conferenza stampa alla vigilia dell'impegno di Champions League contro l'Inter. Qui le sue considerazioni: "Stiller? So che vorreste la formazione, lui ha recuperato ed è in grado di giocare, vale per tutti gli altri. Contro lo Schalke i ragazzi hanno fatto ciò che gli ho chiesto, soprattutto in alcune fasi della partita. Ci è mancato l'ultimo passaggio, non abbiamo fatto ottime scelte, ma abbiamo comunque creato diverse occasioni. Dobbiamo capire che se avessimo per caso segnato un gol l'impressione dei media sarebbe stata diversa. Veniamo da uno 0-0 e di sicuro vorremmo vincere di più e non perdere ancora tanti punti per strada. Abbiamo anche da pensare alla Champions League, ma avremmo dovuto avere almeno due punti in più in Europa se pensiamo a quanto fatto in casa dell'Atletico. La sfortuna ci ha accompagnato nel percorso, speriamo di riuscire a fare meglio domani".

Undav non ha brillato contro lo Schalke. "Non è solamente lui il tema, sono tutti sempre in discussione. Che lui polarizzi lo sappiamo, ma non voglio parlare di un singolo. Non abbiamo segnato contro lo Schalke, ma è una responsabilità che riguarda tutti".

Che impressione ti fa questo stadio?"Siamo contentissimi di essere qui, abbiamo fatto un giro nello stadio ed è stato impressionante. E' alto, enorme, come lo ricordavo quando l'ho visto da spettatore da adolescente. Guardo tante partite in tv, ho visto anche il derby. Questo stadio ha una storia pazzesca, incluso il capitolo dei mondiali del '90. Siamo felici di poter giocare qui ed essere parte della storia di questo stadio. E' fantastico anche senza tifosi, non vediamo l'ora di goderci l'atmosfera domani sera".

Che ricordi hai della prima partita vista qui?"Sì, di fatto ho visto qui la prima partita in cui ho preso consapevolezza del calcio. Abbiamo visto la partita della Germania qui nel '90 e da allora la passione è cresciuta dentro di me".

Ti aspettavi di venire qui ad affrontare l'Inter con un punto in più in classifica?"Devo essere molto sincero, era impossibile aspettarselo. siamo davanti e faremo di tutto perché l'Inter rimanga dietro. Ci vogliamo misurare contro loro, sono tra le più grandi squadre in Europa. Noi siamo in grado di tenere testa anche ad avversarie così forti".

Ha visto Atalanta-Inter?"Ho visto Atalanta-Inter live, ma anche altre partite in differita. L'Inter è organizzata benissimo, ha un grandissimo allenatore e grandi individualità. Riesce a trovare una commistione tra la grande organizzazione, con schemi molto chiari, e il valore dei singoli. Vedendola da lontano la vedo come un unico collettivo che funziona molto bene, ha tutte le corde accordate quando i singoli si muovono d'insieme".

Si parla delle amnesie difensive dell'Inter in Italia."Abbiamo visto che per l'Inter è insolito prendere così tanti gol, non so se ci siano falle strutturali. Non penso che l'Inter giocherà con la stessa difesa di Bergamo. Ci sono anche delle defezioni, ma hanno una squadra fantastica e noi dobbiamo mostrare di cosa siamo capaci, sfruttando magari i loro punti deboli. Vogliamo portare la nostra proposta di calcio, si tratterà di cogliere momenti che saranno magari più favorevoli per noi e sfruttarli al meglio".

Il momento dell'Inter può favorirvi in qualche modo?"Abbiamo visto tutti insieme la partita contro l'Atalanta, osservando i loro meccanismi e tenendo in considerazione ogni aspetto. Vediamo che cercano di giocare di sponda sulla punta, magari domani giocherà Lautaro, ma hanno anche grande tecnica da abbinare alla fisicità. Dimarco ha un piede sinistro che non devo neanche raccontarvi, per esempio. Abbiamo una grande sfida davanti a noi: tenere conto di tutte le componenti dell'Inter".

#2143. Stoccarda, Chabot: "Giocare contro l'Inter a San Siro incredibile. Lautaro e Thuram..."

Stoccarda, Chabot: "Giocare contro l'Inter a San Siro incredibile. Lautaro e Thuram..."

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

Non solo Hoeness in conferenza stampa per lo Stoccarda alla vigilia dell'impegno di Champions League contro l'Inter. Qui le considerazioni di Jeff Chabot, difensore del club tedesco: "Io sono molto contento, non vedo l'ora di scendere in campo contro l'Inter in questo stadio. Abbiamo fatto grandi prestazioni in Champions League, speriamo di dare continuità anche domani. Qui al Meazza ho già giocato, è stata un'esperienza fantastica. Volevo addirittura venire a vedere il derby durante la pandemia, ma purtroppo si giocava a porte chiuse. Giocare queste partite qui comunque è qualcosa di incredibile".

Le esperienze in Italia con Sampdoria e Spezia?"Mi hanno aiutato molto, ho avuto qui i miei inizi. Ho avuto tanti allenatori, un po' di alti e bassi, ma mi hanno reso forte. Il mio italiano purtroppo non è più così buono (ride, ndr)".

Prima di giocare contro Fullkrug ti eri preparato. Hai fatto lo stesso?"Abbiamo fatto delle analisi, ovviamente conosciamo le loro caratteristiche. Thuram lo conosco da quando giocava in Germania, Lautaro anche lo conosciamo tutti e non servono parole. Sarà molto difficile fermarli. L'approccio deve essere collettivo ovviamente, poi saremo noi a livello individuale a dover fare la differenza".

Cosa non è andato nell'ultima partita?"Tante cose non sono state da noi. Di solito cerchiamo di attaccare e difendere insieme, bisogna capire quando e come fare le due fasi. Cercheremo di fare meglio domani sera. Abbiamo grandi difensori, ogni volta dobbiamo giocarci il posto. Bisogna vedere chi è nello stato di forma migliore, importante è schierare una formazione al 100%".

#2142. Europa League, ecco quanti punti servono ai rossoneri per qualificarsi ai play-off: e giovedì c’è la bolgia di Atene

Congelato momentaneamente il campionato dopo l'ultima vittoria casalinga per 5-1 contro il Frosinone, per i rossoneri è tempo di concentrarsi sull'Europa League. La classifica è di quelle veramente brutte da guardare: il Milan si trova alla 29° posizione con soli 3 punti conquistati al giro di boa della League Phase. La situazione è negativa, sì, ma non irrecuperabile. A quattro giornate dalla fine appare ormai molto difficile l'accesso diretto agli ottavi di finale, al contrario invece i play-off rimangono ampiamente alla portata.

Ma quanti punti servono al club meneghino per giocarsi l'accesso agli ottavi di finale nel doppio confronto dei play-off? Considerando la classifica attuale, i rossoneri potrebbero entrare nelle prime 24 posizionil con 9 punti. Attualmente l'ultima posizione utile è occupata dallo Sturm Graz (24°) che insieme a Hoffenheim (23°) e Sparta Praga (22°) sono a 4 punti. 9 punti però, potrebbero anche non bastare in caso di determinate combinazioni quindi, per stare sicuri, ai rossoneri servono ancora due vittorie ed un pareggio nelle prossime 4 per non rischiare un primo grosso fallimento stagionale.

#2141. Inter, vietato sbagliare: la Champions non ammette altri passi falsi. E Chivu si affida a Pio

Inter, vietato sbagliare: la Champions non ammette altri passi falsi. E Chivu si affida a Pio

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

Scegli Fcinter1908.it come tuo sito preferito su Google: clicca qui

Dopo il rocambolesco pareggio in casa dell’Atalanta, l’Inter si concentra sulla Champions League e sulla sfida contro lo Stoccarda. Per sperare di evitare i playoff servono 6 punti nelle prossime due sfide.

“Se il Como è la sorpresa italiana, l’Inter è la versione in negativo: il nostro club dalla dimensione più europea, due finali negli ultimi quattro anni, lo scudetto sulla maglia e una rosa ampia, in Champions ha un po’ perso la bussola rispetto ai tempi di Inzaghi. Sette sconfitte in otto partite, compresi i playoff con il Bodo nella scorsa edizione, e i due ko con Real Madrid e Bruges in questa. Si temeva il peggio. Inversione di tendenza nelle ultime due gare, due successi consecutivi contro lo Shakhtar e poi in trasferta con il Feyenoord, non scontato. La classifica non è più drammatica, i punti però sono ancora 6, e non 9 come da previsioni: il quindicesimo posto assicura al momento i playoff in prima fascia, ma è vietato perdere altri colpi o sono guai”, spiega La Gazzetta dello Sport.

“Domani a San Siro contro l’imprevedibile Stoccarda (7 punti), e all’ultima a Brastislava contro lo Slovan (l’unico a quota zero), l’Inter deve conquistare 6 punti. Come per il Como, quota 12 è l’obiettivo minimo, perché in mezzo ci sono i due big match contro il Borussia (Dortmund) e il Liverpool (San Siro), repliche delle sfide dell’anno scorso: ko in casa con i Reds, successo in Germania con i gialloneri. L’Inter di coppa di una volta non resterebbe a secco. L’ultima lascia qualche perplessità. Lo Stoccarda non è facile da leggere, pari con l’Atletico e a terra sul campo del Galatasaray. Ma l’altro avversario dell’Inter è in casa: Calha infortunato, quindi Chivu deve scegliere un regista, e Lautaro non ancora in condizione. Inter nella grazia di Pio”, aggiunge il quotidiano.

#2140. Raul Jimenez’s late penalty seals share of spoils for Wolves against Blackburn

An injury-time penalty from returning hero Raul Jimenez ensured Wolves came away with a point in their Championship opener after Blackburn had produced a comeback.

Wolves led through Marshall Munetsi’s cool finish on 18 minutes only for Sean McLoughlin’s first Blackburn goal to draw the visitors level on 27 minutes with a well-timed run and header.

The visitors turned the game around on 48 minutes through Andri Gudjohnsen’s close-range strike only for substitute Jimenez to level the game three minutes into injury time from the spot after Ryan Alebiosu handled the ball in the box. Jimenez and his wife marked his return to Molineux by giving 5,000 fans black scarves emblazoned with the words ‘Si Senor’ - a nod to his song - and Jimenez did not disappoint as he rifled in his penalty.

LATE DRAMA! 😱Raul Jimenez cooly converts from the penalty spot, deep into added time to save a point for Wolves 🔥 pic.twitter.com/5h2lMxcHNU

Cesar Peixoto wants Wolves to play with their heads rather than their hearts after the home side rescued a late point in the first game of the Championship season following relegation. Wolves replaced Rob Edwards with Portuguese coach Peixoto during the summer and he felt his side’s loss of organisation allowed Blackburn to take control of the game.

Peixoto said: “One point is not what we want, we want three points. We lost some organisation in the second half and played more with our hearts and not our heads and brains.

“We have to keep our mental stability during the 90 minutes because we’re better when we’re organised. We need to play our way with our identity. It’s a lesson for us to understand when we lose organisation it’s more difficult for us and more easy for the opposition.

“We’re working hard to bring more solutions [into the club] but we need to work hard with the players we have. It’s the first game, there’s a lot to work on and a lot to improve.”

Tony Mowbray took charge of Blackburn in the league for the first time since returning to the club for a second stint in the dugout. Rovers recorded their lowest league finish since Mowbray’s first stint in charge of the club last term but the Blackburn boss is confident his side have enough to beat the drop and climb up the table this campaign.

Mowbray said: “Ultimately, it feels like a defeat, it’s a bit disappointing and a bit frustrating. I just felt a lot of decisions were starting to swing their way. It’s harsh to take, I don’t think they deserved a second goal. We asked the players to be brave and we were collectively very strong.

“Everybody has us relegated, probably on the back of finishing 20th last season and losing a couple of our better players and not spending much money, which is a good motivation for the team. We deserved to win the game, we showed there’s enough quality to give teams problems and I hope there’s help on the way [in the transfer window].”

The first chance of the season came the way of Blackburn as a cross-field pass found Alebiosu down the right, who floated a ball into the box only for an unmarked Oladipo Afolayan to head wide from eight-yards out.

However, Wolves scored the first goal of the new campaign when Fer Lopez played a defence-splitting through ball into the path of Munetsi, who coolly found the net with a first time effort from the edge of the penalty area.

"A vibrant Molineux rejoices!" 🎉Marshall Munetsi cooly slots home for Wolves to give them the lead on the opening night of the Championship season 🥶 pic.twitter.com/tVHdbNv3Hq

Blackburn drew level through an unlikely source just nine minutes later as McLoughlin headed in his first-ever goal in English football in more than 150 games when he met Ryoya Morishita’s cross from the right at the near post.

The home side had chances to retake the lead before the break as Lopez curled a shot narrowly over the top from 20-yards while Kieran Trippier teed up Mateus Mane for a chance as the teenager found the top of the net from distance.

Blackburn turned the game on its head minutes after the break as Gudjohnsen turned home Afolayan’s deflected ball in from the right from close range to stun Molineux into silence.

"It's brilliantly worked by Blackburn Rovers!" 💥Andri Guðjohnsen finishes from close range to turn the game around and the visitors the lead 👏 pic.twitter.com/vkKQQezicK

Peixoto introduced Jimenez from the bench on the hour mark but even the Mexican could not lift Wolves with Blackburn comfortable for long periods as frustration grew in the stands.

However, Alebiosu handled Jackson Tchatchoua’s cross from the left in injury time and the referee awarded a spot kick which allowed Jimenez to mark his Wolves return with an equaliser from 12-yards as he sent Balazs Toth the wrong way.

Wolverhampton: Bentley, Trippier, Mosquera, Toti Gomes, Wolfe, Lopez, Andre Trindade, Munetsi, Rodrigo Gomes, Armstrong, Mane.Subs used: Jiménez 60 (Mateus Mané), ‌Doyle ‌67 (Rodrigo Gomes), ⁠Tchatchoua 67 (Trippier), Pedro Lima 79 (André),Chirewa 79 (Møller Wolfe)Scorers: M. “I think it is a really harsh one. He [the referee] has to be sure at such a critical time of the game. And I do not think he can be sure. I think he has almost had a guess that he is in the box. It is such a big call.”

“We see the ref’s position. The fact the ref makes the decision to give the penalty in my opinion, it is a bigger decision to give it then to not. We have got the luxury of the replays and we are still not sure. His whole body is outside the box.”

“It looks outside the box to me [the penalty incident]. The still picture suggests to me it was outside the box. We can get better and we will have to. We have to be more dominant with the ball at times and be better in attack. We have done a lot of work on that in pre-season. Having been in this league a long time, the power of the dressing room is crucial and any motivation tool you can you use is important. I think we will be fine. You have to enjoy football and love it. I think the boys enjoyed it tonight.”

“Obviously frustrated. The boys did everything to get the three points but it is gutting in the end. I thought I was outside the box when the ball came in and the decision went against us. They are saying it is in the box and it is frustrating. We dug deep and thought we had it. I cannot fault everyone as we all put in a shift, it was just the final details with the penalty decision. We deserved the three points. If we play like that every week, we will go far in the table.”

Raul Jimenez reacts after his late penalty saved a point for Wolves against Blackburn 🗣️ pic.twitter.com/aXvVeWw6fc

“Before the game we were predicting Wolves would be right up there [this season] and we thought it would be a comfortable win. But that was not the case.  Blackburn gave a really good account of themselves and had lots of openings. [Cesar] Peixoto will be breathing a massive sigh of relief with that handball.”

“Wolves got away with one if I am being really honest. In terms of their second-half performance, they did not do anywhere near enough. I thought Blackburn were excellent throughout and deservedly took the lead. It is a huge decision [to give the penalty], but in the end Raul Jimenez steps up to get his side the point. [Cesar Peixoto] will be delighted to get something in the end.”

When Wolverhampton Wanderers last attacked the Championship under a Portuguese manager new to English football, it proved a vintage season.

Molineux was under the spell of Nuno Espírito Santo in that 2017-18 campaign, and the big question is whether César Peixoto can lead a repeat nearly a decade later.

Peixoto was a surprise appointment in June, replacing Rob Edwards, and he has been entrusted with the mission of returning Wolves to the Premier League at the first time of asking.

“I have changed some things with set-pieces and some things in other moments of the game because I want the team to be organised and understand all the different moments within a match. During pre-season we have scored some goals from high pressing, but we will not be able to press high in every moment of every game. The players have to understand the game and recognise what each moment requires. It is important that we work as a team. When we can press high, we’ll press high. When we need to defend in a mid-block, we will stay organised. When the opposition pushes us deeper, which will sometimes happen, we have to be organised there as well.

“There are a lot of crosses in the Championship, for example, so the team needs to understand what to do in those situations. We want to play our football, be offensive and react strongly when we lose the ball, but we also need to understand the different moments of the game. I think we are at a good level to attack the Championship.”

Our experts have been having their say ahead of the new Championship season. A number of them believe Wolves will be promoted, including John Percy:

It is difficult to look beyond West Ham. Nuno Espírito Santo romped to the Championship title with Wolves in 2018 and remains a shrewd operator (along with his No 2 Rui Pedro Silva), while convincing Jarrod Bowen to stay is significant. I am going to go for Wolves and Middlesbrough as the other two promoted clubs.

The boys are in the Black Country 👋 #WOLvROV | #Rovers 🔵⚪️ pic.twitter.com/WjuGwrxYtQ

The 2026/27 Championship season kicks off at Molineux as Wolves host Blackburn, with the home side hoping to put the bad memories of last season firmly behind them. There are new playoff rules in place for this season, with six places now up for grabs in the play-offs.

Wolves are aiming to return to the Premier League at the first attempt, having endured a miserable season in the top flight last year as they finished bottom with just three wins to their name. It has been a summer of change at the club as they chose to change managers. Rob Edwards lost his job in what was a shock move and was replaced by 46-year-old Cesar Peixoto, with this being his first head coaching position outside of his home country Portugal. Back in 2017/18, Wolves won the Championship title led by Peixoto’s compatriot Nuno Espirito Santo.

“I am ready,” Peixoto told Wolves’ club website. “We have worked very hard during pre-season, and I think we have had a great pre-season. All the players have worked very, very hard and they understand what I want from them. I am ready and confident going into the first game. Football is football, whether it is in Portugal or England, but there are different characteristics to the game here. We have watched, observed and analysed the Championship. We did that work before I came to the club, so we know what to expect.

“It is going to be a tough season with good teams, but we are prepared to fight for the three points game after game and week after week. We know what we want from the team and we know what our big goal is. We’re going to work very hard and fight to achieve that goal.”

They have brought in vast experience on the player side in the shape of Kieran Trippier and Raul Jimenez, who is back at Molineux three years after leaving for Fulham. Although they have lost Brazilian midfielder Joao Gomes to Aston Villa, most of their squad has stayed intact as they pursue promotion back to the top flight. It is a special year for the club as they celebrate their 150th anniversary.

Blackburn, led by experienced manager Tony Mowbray, did not enjoy a great season in the Championship last year, finishing down in 20th place and just five points above the relegation zone. 14 years have now passed since Blackburn last played in the top flight.

“I think it is a tough start,” Mowbray said. “On paper, I would suggest the first couple of games, particularly, jumped out as a tough start. One of the Premier League sides from last season, away from home. They have got a very talented squad, a pretty expensive squad. They have got some very good individual talent. Yet, there is an argument to say they have not won many football matches in the last calendar year. So, what we get, we will have to wait and see.”

These sides last met in the FA Cup during the 2024/25 season, with Wolves winning that fourth-round tie 2-0 thanks to goals from Matheus Cunha and Gomes, both of whom are now plying their trade elsewhere. The omens would appear to be on Wolves’ side as they are unbeaten in their last eight league meetings with Blackburn. Both sides progressed to the second round of the League Cup last weekend, with Wolves beating Port Vale 3-0 and Blackburn overcoming Burton Albion 2-1.

#2135. Mignani ha risvegliato il Catanzaro: due vittorie in tre giorni e ultimo posto addio

Quattro punti nelle prime otto giornate, otto nelle successive cinque. Sono numeri che certificano non solo la risalita del Catanzaro in classifica, che ha abbandonato questa sera un ultimo posto che occupava praticamente da agosto, ma anche il lavoro svolto da Michele Mignani subentrato a fine ottobre a Giorgio Gorgone.

Da quel 6-1 subito in casa contro l’Ascoli, che ha segnato la fine dell’esperienza dell’ex tecnico del Pescara, di cose ne sono cambiate. Mignani non ha cercato subito rivoluzioni mantenendo la difesa a tre e rinforzando il centrocampo con un uomo in più e l’attacco mettendo un’altra punta al fianco del totem Iemmello. Un 3-5-2 più solido e compatto che dopo un inizio ancora a rilento, due punti nelle prime tre gare della nuova gestione, ha iniziato a decollare in quella che poteva essere la sfida più dura: quella contro il Palermo capolista e imbattuto. Il sorprendente 3-1 sui rosanero aveva riportato la vittoria in casa giallorossa dopo oltre due mesi (l’ultima volta fu con la Carrarese il 12 settembre) e probabilmente dato una scarica di adrenalina alla squadra che pochi giorni dopo ha replicato lo stesso risultato questa volta in casa della Juve Stabia, conquistando la prima vittoria esterna della stagione.

Il nuovo tecnico ha saputo valorizzare anche quei giocatori che finora avevano trovato meno spazio o non erano apparsi brillanti come per esempio l'attaccante Emanuele Pecorino, autore di due gol nelle ultime due gare, che stasera non ha fatto rimpiangere il capitano Iemmello. Ma anche i gemelli Tchaouna o il centrocampista Giovane sembrano essersi ritrovati dopo dei primi mesi poco felici.

#2133. RILEGGI LIVE – Manchester City-Napoli, De Bruyne: “Situazione sgradevole nei confronti della mia famiglia. Futuro? C’è poco da dire”

Termina la conferenza stampa di De Bruyne

De Bruyne: “A me piace giocare a calcio, non dobbiamo soltanto lanciare lungo, dobbiamo avere coraggio. Conosco Maresca e come fa giocare le sue squadre. Il caso del Man City? Sono successe tante cose da quando ero qui, sono molto fiero, voglio tanto bene al presidente e ho parlato spesso coi proprietari del club. Ho solo affetto per questo club, i tifosi mi hanno trattato in maniera eccezionale per 10 anni”.

De Bruyne: “Il mio futuro? Posso dire ben poco. Ho giocato al 400% finora, non ho più vent’anni, ne ho trentasei, e ci sono tante cose da prendere in considerazione e c’è ben poco da dire. Sto giocando bene e sto cercando di aiutare la squadra. Con il Belgio il modo di giocare sono più abituato, è più facile adattarmi a quello stile di gioco, ma ho l’impressione di giocare bene anche con il Napoli, ho fatto il mio dovere per la squadra. Vedo poche differenze tra quello che faccio con il Belgio e con il Napoli. Gioco da titolare quasi tutte le partite, quindi cosa posso chiedere di più”.

De Bruyne: “Ho giocato in tante posizioni nella mia carriera, ho cambiato spesso ruolo. Ma il ruolo è sempre dinamico nel calcio, gioco in base al mio sentimento, in base a dove trovo spazio. E penso di aver giocato bene finora, poco importa dove gioco. Sono dispiaciuto, perché se leggeste l’articolo, cosa che non avete fatto, perché io ero fuori Belgio e ci sono state delle dichiarazioni di mia moglie. So che i giornalisti in Italia hanno creato questa situazione e non so perché. Voi non conoscete la mia vita. Non avete diritto di esprimervi sulla mia vita privata. È stata una situazione sgradevole per me e per mia moglie”.

Kevin De Bruyne, centrocampista del Napoli, parlerà in conferenza stampa alla vigilia del match contro il Manchester City.
